DEAD.** The reviewer asked the one blocking question — *“what is the ±13V5 decay time constant after input power is lost?”* — having found that the bias cap has **three** jobs, not two, and that the third (**arrival time**) wants it SMALLER and has **no stated requirement** | **TERM 4 FOUND, ENUMERATED, AND SMALL — the model is now CLOSED BY COUNT (M31a).** The reviewer predicted a fourth omission within a day of M31 and found one: **input bulk sustains the upstream converter to UVLO, and ENERGY (not charge) crosses a switching stage.** They bounded it at **1.4–51 ms** on *assumed* 220–1000 µF and refused to compute a v4 from imagined inputs. **Enumerated here: `VBATT_PROT` has 10 + 22 = 32 µF and nothing larger exists in the path → 2.47 mJ → 0.2–1.6 ms.** **F108 v3 stands.** *Original:* **MODEL v3, AND NOW A MEASUREMENT INSTEAD (M31 / `BRINGUP.md` 4b.7).** Corrected a SECOND time by the reviewer: **in dropout the LDO pass element is ~0.52 Ω — a short — so both caps are in PARALLEL and phase 2 has 54.6 µF, not 22.6 µF.** Hold-up **3.96–16.87 ms**, margin **2–19×**. **The conclusion survives and they declined to reopen it — but the number has been modelled three times and corrected twice, both times by omitting stored energy, both times flattering the conclusion, so it is now a BENCH MEASUREMENT rather than a fourth calculation.** *Original:* **HELD AND RE-COMPUTED 2026-08-22 after the reviewer caught a SAFE-DIRECTION ERROR in it — my first figure counted the LDO OUTPUT capacitance only, and an LDO keeps regulating until its INPUT reaches dropout.** *M10: the error was in the direction that made my own conclusion look stronger, which is the direction nobody audits.* **Corrected, including upstream: ±15V5_A carries 22 µF + 10 µF = 32 µF, and headroom before LT3045 dropout is 15.5 − 13.5 − 0.26 = 1.74 V** → regulated hold-up **0.28–1.19 ms**, then output decay **1.53–6.52 ms**, total **1.80–7.71 ms**. **THE CONCLUSION SURVIVES AND THE MARGIN IS 4–42×, not 5–45×.** *Their hypothetical 151 µF at a 24 V SEPIC does not apply — the LDOs are fed from ±15V5_A, not from the SEPIC output, so the headroom is 1.74 V rather than 10.2 V. Reaching their 33 ms onset would need **888–3793 µF upstream** against the 32 µF drawn.* **AND §6.4 DOES ASSIGN THE SHUNT TO THE COLLAPSE WINDOW, which settles their Reading A vs B:** *“**Failure behaviour is the product of two independent mechanisms.** Rails collapse → LED dies → series opens. **Independently, the −13V5-derived bias dies → shunt closes.**”* — that is the shunt, named, in the rail-collapse case, under a heading about failure behaviour. **But their reduction is still the right SIZE and I have taken it: the finding is not ‘the shunt is useless’, it is “§6.4 asserts two mechanisms and one of them has a latency that is nowhere stated.”** *§6.4 says both things — a steady-state job ({‘kill `C_off` leakage’}) and a collapse-window role — and the latency bites only on the second.* *Original figure:* **Computed from the drawn capacitance: 22 µF/25 V bulk at the LT3045/LT3094 output plus 6 × 100 nF OPA1656 decoupling per rail ≈ 22.6 µF.** Against the §7.1 budget (200 mA) that is **1.53 ms** to collapse; against OPA1656 quiescent alone (46.8 mA) **6.52 ms**. **Versus the shunt's onset at 33–75 ms and its specified `R_DS(ON)` at ~176 ms (F107/R-66).** **SO THE SEQUENCE IS: relay opens ≤ 0.4 ms → op-amp rails collapse 1.5–6.5 ms → shunt begins conducting 33–75 ms.** **The series element does the entire job, 4–17× before the rails die; the shunt closes 5–45× after there is anything left to suppress.** *Exposure window is the ~1–6 ms between relay-open and rail-collapse, and the shunt is absent for all of it.* **CONSEQUENCE FOR §6.4: the ‘second independent fail-safe mechanism’ does not operate during the event §7.4 says it exists for.** That is not an argument that the FET is wrong — it is an argument that **its stated justification has never been checked against a time**, which is exactly R-66's point and F98's shape one level up. **Assumptions named: linear constant-current discharge; bulk capacitance as drawn (I found no other caps on these rails); op-amp behaviour below its minimum supply not modelled.** **This makes option C's ‘no arrival delay’ entry stronger than the reviewer claimed — a permanent resistor is present at t = 0, and on these numbers the FET shunt is present at no useful time at all** |
| **F107** | ✅ | **R-65 ANSWERED FROM OUR OWN COPPER, AND THE OVERLAP DOES NOT OCCUR — but the reviewer's structural point survives the closure and is the first entry on option C's side of the ledger.** They asked: *is there a specified order between series-open and shunt-close, and what enforces it?* — warning that if the shunt closes first, a **DN2540 pair (50 Ω) drives the OPA1656 to 125.6 mA pk against `I_SC` ±100 mA typ**, hard-clipping **during the very interval §12 measures** | **ANSWERED, TWO WAYS, AND BOTH ARE SAFE.** **(1) In a COMMANDED mute the overlap cannot exist at all:** the shunt has **no MCU control** — `MUTE_BIAS_N` is a fixed 100k/100k divider from `−13V5_A` with 1 µF to ground, sitting at **−6.75 V**, so while the rails are up the bias holds and **the shunt never closes.** Only the series element operates. **(2) In a RAIL COLLAPSE, break-before-make is enforced by the RC, not by sequencing:** τ = (100k∥100k)×1 µF = **50 ms**, so the pair begins conducting **33–75 ms** after rail loss (V_GS(off) −3.5…−1.5 V) against the TLP3475's `t_OFF` **≤ 0.4 ms** — **the relay opens 82–188× faster than the shunt closes.** **THEIR POINT SURVIVES ANYWAY AND I HAVE KEPT IT:** *“being switched means having a transition, and a transition has an order, and an order is a thing that can be got wrong.”* **Here it is right by two orders of magnitude — but by an RC that was chosen for bias filtering, not for ordering.** *That is §8.11's shared-margin case again: the 1 µF now defends a break-before-make guarantee nobody wrote down.* **Back-reference owed at the bias network.** **And note the tension they exposed: §6.4 REQUIRES the two mechanisms be independent (*“neither depends on … the other”*) — which is exactly what removes any enforced ordering. The fail-safe property and the ordering guarantee are in structural tension, and the RC resolves it by accident** |

Each pin moves only ±667 mV about 2.561 V (**1.894–3.228 V**), so rail-to-rail output is not required. **Three placements that are not arbitrary and are argued on-sheet:** R<sub>B</sub> returns to `GND` and **not** `AGND_REF`, which is the INA1650's REF *sense* node and must carry no current (T1-67/F36's rule at one remove) · R<sub>BP</sub> is **10 MΩ deliberately**, because anything returning to `VBIAS_ADC` injects signal current into a node shared by four channels whose impedance is ~690 Ω at 20 Hz — at 100 kΩ the crosstalk would be **−33.7 dB** *(prose first said −37 dB — a hand estimate quoted instead of the generator's value; review A7 caught the inconsistency. The sheet now carries the closed form, crosstalk = (N<sub>ch</sub>·NG<sub>N</sub>/2)·Z<sub>bias</sub>/R<sub>BP</sub> with Z<sub>bias</sub> = 5.12 kΩ ∥ 796 Ω = 689 Ω at 20 Hz, so a 40 dB decision is arithmetic on the sheet rather than a number in prose. The reviewer's decomposition of the gap was exactly right: 12.0 dB for four channels + 3.5 dB for leg N's noise gain = 15.6 dB)*; AKM never meets this because *both* their legs are inverting · the legs are **parallel, not cascaded**, because a cascade doubles leg N's distortion differentially where parallel legs add in quadrature. **Supersedes F50's first fix:** driving REF A/B to 2.56 V is single-ended drive by another name and falls to the same arithmetic; REF A/B stay on `AGND_REF` and NOTE3 is unchanged. **ERC 41 → 33**; the four `AINn_N` `pin_not_driven` are gone and the netlist walks INA1650 → ADC pin on all four channels |
| **F50** | ✅ | **CLOSED — and the answer is neither Shape A nor Shape B, it is AKM's own hybrid (T1-103).** **The ratiometric decree is confirmed by the vendor's own arithmetic:** Figure 76's bias divider is **10 kΩ / 10.5 kΩ = 0.51220 × AVDD**, dead centre of the window. **A fixed 2.56 V reference fails**: against AVDD ±2 % it lands at 0.5224 and 0.5020 × AVDD — **outside the window at BOTH ends, with nothing left for divider, offset or drift.** The window is ±1 % on a *ratio*, so only a ratiometric source can hold it. | **The shape AKM uses:** AC coupling sits **between the ±15 V buffer and a single-supply +5 V level-shifting stage**, not at the ADC pins — so the ADC is **DC-driven** from that stage (Shape A's property where it matters) while the DC break happens upstream (Shape B's element where it is cheap). Our INA1650 plays the first-stage buffer; what is missing is AKM's **second stage**, at unity rather than their −14.5 dB, since our 0.94 V<sub>rms</sub> is already at level. **The bias divider stays UNBUFFERED** — it feeds an op-amp non-inverting input, so T1-67's low-impedance rule never engages; stiffness comes from 10 µF ∥ 0.1 µF. **Riders carried:** the new stage is a single-supply CMOS part (OPA2320 / TLV9002 class), **not** an OPA1656; and the ADC pins get AKM's own 10 Ω + 100 pF + 15 nF differential network, which is also where F49's clamp inequality repeats on the ADC side |

As drawn, the INA1650's `REF A/B` tie to `AGND_REF`, so its output common mode is **0 V** — the ADC inputs sit **2.51 V below their specified window**, and the negative half of every waveform swings **below AVSS**. This is not a margin question; it is the wrong operating point. | **The fix is at the REF pins, not at the ADC.** Drive `REF A/B` from a **buffered ~2.56 V** and the INA1650's output common mode lands in the window by construction. **Note what is NOT the fix:** a divider on REF. T1-67 is explicit — REF *"must be driven from a low impedance"* — and F36 drew the distinction the hard way: **FB is inside a loop that regulates it; REF believes whatever it sees.** A divider here would be the REF-pin rule violated at one remove, which is exactly the shape the operator predicted. **Also not `VMID_OUT`**: the INA1650's buffered mid-rail is the mid-point of *its own* ±13V5_A supply, i.e. 0 V — the right pin for the wrong voltage. **Needs a buffer from +5V_A**, and every OPA1656 unit on the DAC sheets is already spoken for, so this is a new part |

| **F35** | ✅ | **CLOSED — split kept. XO→DSP direct, three buffer outputs, `Y3` spare.** The equalisation argument dissolves on inspection: the converters care about BCLK/LRCLK skew **relative to each other**, and those are regenerated by the DSP's serial ports from the PLL — not from MCLK's arrival time. Matching the DSP's MCLK path equalises a delay **nothing downstream can perceive**. The cost side is asymmetric and real: the PLL reference is the root of every clock on the board, so jitter there multiplies through the PLL into everything, while the same jitter on a converter leg touches one device. **When one side of a trade is "no benefit", the error bar on the other side is irrelevant.** `Y3` disposition per datasheet: **"Unused outputs can be left floating"** — annotated as *datasheet-sanctioned*, explicitly not a convenient future tap. *Original text below.* | — |

Driving all four from the buffer (`Y0`→DSP, `Y1`→ADC, `Y2`→DAC1, `Y3`→DAC2) equalises loading and propagation delay across the whole tree and still satisfies §3's "distribute from the XO through a buffer, not daisy-chained". | **Escalated, not applied** — §3 is [HARD] and this is clock-tree topology. Counter-consideration: it inserts the buffer's additive jitter (7.5 fs) into the DSP's PLL reference, where today the DSP sees the XO raw. T3-02's 6-layer guess is now a **floor of 4, not a ceiling**; 6 stays live pending routing reality. Provenance matters here: "the exposed pad note forces it" is a far cleaner basis than "we assumed", and every Block E conversation now starts from 4 as **given** rather than open. *Original text below.* | — |
| ~~**F25-orig**~~ | | *Original:* **a Block E stackup input with teeth.** T1-38 requires identical copper squares on **all layers**, via-stitched, connected to "a **dedicated copper ground layer within the PCB**". On a 2-layer board there is no "within". **The part therefore mandates an internal ground plane** — an argument for >=4 layers arriving from the silicon rather than from routing density, and independent support for T3-02's 6-layer estimate. | Fold into the layout-blocked list alongside the pad's via array. It **strengthens** §6.3's single-unsplit-ground-plane mandate rather than competing with it |

Low urgency; decide before the BOM freezes |

Four devices, four addresses, **no spare in the AKM prefix.** Bus map logged below; the second I2C controller is **not** needed and stays in reserve. | **Logged as §9 bus map.** The zero-slack condition is the thing the next revision must see coming: a fifth AKM part on this bus is impossible without splitting it |
| ~~**F23-orig**~~ | | *Original text retained:* **The AKM parts may not all fit on one I2C bus — which would defeat §5's "strap consistently to I2C".** T1-28: AK5574 and AK4458 have a **2-bit** chip address; AK4118A has C1 fixed at 0, so **one selectable bit**. The bus must carry AK5574 + **two** AK4458 + AK4118A + ADS1015 + TMP102. If the AKM families share a base slave address — and a ragged first-pass extraction showed the same `00100` prefix near all three — the two DACs plus the ADC plus the SPDIF receiver may not be uniquely addressable. | **Extract the exact slave-address bytes for all three AKM parts and lay them out against each other before capture.** If they collide, the escape is to put one part on 3-wire serial instead (all three support it), which *contradicts* §5's [VERIFY] instruction to strap consistently to I2C — so it is an operator decision, not a workaround |
| **F24** | ⚠ | **NEW. The LT3045 input rails are unspecified, and +5V_A cannot come from +5V_D.** §7.1 has always said "+5V_A / +3V3_A — LT3045-class ultra-low-noise LDOs" without naming what feeds them. An LDO needs headroom; **+5V_D at 5.0 V cannot produce a clean +5V_A**. It needs a higher intermediate rail, or derivation from ±13V5_A at a cost of (13.5 − 5) × I dissipated in the LDO. | Resolve before capture. Flagged in v0.5 §7.1 note 4 rather than silently inventing a rail |
| **F22** | ✅ | **CLOSED in v0.5 — the MCLK fanout was on the wrong rail, twice over.** T1-30: the LMK1C110x is a 1.8/2.5/3.3 V part, so §7.1's placement of "fanout" on **+5V_D** was wrong on its face. And it is the most jitter-critical device in the [HARD] §3 clock tree, with its 7.5 fs additive-jitter figure specified on a clean supply — so it belongs on **+3V3_A**, not the +3V3_D switcher. Moved to +3V3_A | — |
| **F20** | ⛔ | **NEW. §7.2 step 3 cannot work as written — the control port is dead while the PLL locks.** The [HARD] turn-on sequence says "ESP32 releases DSP reset, slave-boots program over SPI, verifies readback". But T1-23: while the PLL is locking, the SPI slave port is **inactive and every write fails** — up to **10.666 ms**, typically ~3.5 ms. Firmware that releases reset and immediately begins the slave boot has every write silently discarded. **Same shape as F1** — a [HARD] sequence omitting a hardware precondition, failing in a way that looks like a wiring fault. | Insert a step between current 3 and 4: after releasing reset, **configure the PLL (0xF000-0xF003), then poll `PLL_LOCK` 0xF004 Bit 0 until set, before any program write.** Needs operator sign-off as a [HARD] amendment |
| **F19** | ⛔ | **NEW. The DSP core draws far more than §7.1 anticipates, and it lands on +3V3_D.** T1-20: **495 mA maximum, 220 mA typical** at 1.2 V. Because DVDD derives from IOVDD, that current is drawn **from the 3.3 V rail**, and the 3.3->1.2 V drop dissipates **~1 W at maximum program** wherever the pass element sits. §7.1 offers "+3V3_D - buck **or LDO** from 5 V": with ~500 mA for the DSP plus the ESP32-S3 (several hundred mA peak on WiFi TX) plus BM83 plus IOVDD's own 53 mA, +3V3_D is a **1 A-class rail**, and an LDO from 5 V would burn ~2 W. | **+3V3_D must be a buck, not an LDO** — the "or LDO" option should be struck. Also feeds a thermal requirement §9 does not currently carry |
| **F18** | ⚠ | **NEW. §7.1's "+1V2_CORE - LDO from 3V3" is one of two valid methods, and the datasheet's own is the other.** T1-21: the on-chip regulator is a **controller driving an external PNP pass transistor** (`VDRIVE`, pin 3), rated to 800 mA. Real tradeoff: **the PNP path** moves ~1 W into a discrete you can give copper to rather than into an LDO package, but T1-22 imposes supply ordering (DVDD after AVDD/IOVDD/PVDD). **The external-LDO path** has *"no special sequence required"* but puts ~1 W in the LDO. | Operator's call — it trades thermal placement freedom against sequencing complexity, and both interact with the [HARD] §7.2. Not decided unilaterally |
| **F21** | ⚠ | **NEW. `SELFBOOT` (pin 34) needs a deliberate strap.** T1-25: floating = SPI self-boot, 10 kOhm pull-down = I2C self-boot, tied to GND = no self-boot. The 25AA1024 is DNP on v1 (T1-D22) and the ESP32 is boot master, so **the intended state is tied to GND** — but the pin has a 250 kOhm internal pull-up, so leaving it unpopulated **silently selects SPI self-boot from an absent EEPROM**. | Fit a resistor option so self-boot can be enabled later without a cut: GND strap fitted by default, pull-down/float alternatives as DNP pads |
| **F17** | ✅ | **CLOSED - CONFIRMED, and worse than suspected.** T1-16: the ordering guide lists six orderable models and **every one is 72-lead LFCSP `CP-72-6`**. There is **no LQFP *and no WLCSP*** — so §2's "(WLCSP/LQFP - prefer 72-LQFP for hand-rework)" is wrong on both counts and the hand-rework rationale has no package to rest on. Consequences: hot air with preheat, not an iron; stencil aperture and thermal vias for the 5.3 mm exposed pad, which is a **Block E stackup input we do not have**; T2-03's "no BGA" survives, "hand-rework friendly" does not. **Needs an operator decision, not just a note** — it changes the assembly plan. *Original finding:* **the ADAU1466 appears to have no LQFP option.** `REQUIREMENTS.md` §2 specifies "(WLCSP/LQFP — prefer 72-LQFP for hand-rework)". Two independent sources describe the ADAU1462/ADAU1466 as **72-lead 10 × 10 mm LFCSP with a 5.3 mm exposed pad**, and the orderable part `ADAU1466WBCPZ300` carries ADI's `CP` package code, which is LFCSP — `ST` would be LQFP. If this holds: **the hand-rework rationale in §2 does not survive.** LFCSP is leadless, so there are no leads to touch with an iron; it needs hot air with preheat, and the exposed pad needs a paste stencil aperture and thermal vias. It also puts a thermal-via requirement into the stackup, which is a **Block E** input we do not have. T2-03's "no BGA" claim survives — LFCSP is not BGA — but "0.5 mm pitch, hand-rework friendly" does not. | **T2, pending V-01.** Not acted on. The whole point of V-01 is to settle this from the document; two web sources plus a decoded part suffix is strong but is not the datasheet. Raise with the operator once V-01 opens — it may change the package choice, the assembly plan, or both |
| **F16** | ✅ | **CLOSED — ADS1015-class I2C ADC on I2C0; §9 dividers unchanged, only the converter moves; −13V5 divider referenced to a positive rail** → T1-D36. Separately: **the −13.5 V rail cannot be measured by a plain divider to ground** — it needs a divider referenced to a positive rail to bring the tap into the ADC's range. | Q32. |

## 9. I2C0 bus map

**Re-emitted complete, 2026-08-16, as the gate on `09_io`'s close** — named debt twice is a
ledger; three times is a pattern.

| Device | Addr | Strap | Sheet |
|---|---|---|---|
| **AK5574** ADC | **0x10** | `CAD1`=0 (pin 34→GND), `CAD0`=0 (pin 32→GND) | 06 |
| **AK4458 #1** (AOUT1–6) | **0x11** | `CAD1`=0 (pin 12→GND), `CAD0`=1 (pin 15→+3V3_D) | 07 |
| **AK4458 #2** (AOUT7–12) | **0x12** | `CAD1`=0, `CAD0`=1 — *see note* | 08 |
| ~~0x13~~ | — | **freed by F26** when the AK4118A was deleted | — |
| **PCF8574T** GPIO expander | **0x20** | `A2:A1:A0` = 0,0,0 (pins 3,2,1 → GND) | 06 |
| **ADS1015** rail monitor | **0x48** | `ADDR`→GND | 01 |
| **TMP461** temp | **0x49** | `A0`→+3V3_D, `A1`→GND | 01 |

**⛔ The re-emission found a defect, which is the argument for the ritual.** The generator hands
**both** AK4458s the same strap pair `(+3V3_D, GND)` — the `i2c_addr_strap` parameter differs
between the two `build()` calls, but only in the value I passed, and **I passed the same one
twice**. Device #2 therefore also strapped to **0x11**, which is an address collision: two DACs
answering together, presenting as "the second DAC ignores its filter config" — F30's symptom,
different cause. **Fixed:** #2 takes `CAD1`=1 (pin 12 → +3V3_D), `CAD0`=0 → **0x12**.

**Two structural facts worth keeping:** the `0b00100` prefix holds exactly four AKM devices, so a
future ODSP-16's third AK4458 has nowhere to live and I2C1 comes out of reserve at that point,
not before. And 0x20 sits clear of both the AKM block (0x10–0x13) and the monitor pair
(0x48/0x49), so the expander was free to add.

**Pull-ups: 2.2 kΩ on SDA and SCL, on `03_control`, ONCE.** A second pair at the far end is a
rise-time bug that measures fine on a bench and fails at temperature.
